# Skills · S · part 34

789 entries, scrnaseq-count-matrix-simulation to scylladb-data-modeling. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **scrnaseq-count-matrix-simulation**: Use when you need a simulated scRNA-seq gene-by-cell count matrix to validate penalized Latent Dirichlet Allocation (pLDA) topic modeling, or to sanity-check topic-word probability estimation on data with known latent structure. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-count-matrix-simulation--6c2a33f24c96c6a1))
- **scrnaseq-count-simulation**: Use when an evaluation needs synthetic scRNA-Seq count data with known ground-truth cell-cluster labels — e.g. benchmarking clustering or transfer-learning pipelines where ARI can only be computed if true labels exist. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-count-simulation--8de9e1e51e73bf7d))
- **scrnaseq-count-simulation**: Use when benchmarking a cell-cycle or latent-factor removal method on scRNA-Seq data where ground-truth effect assignments (which PCs are cell-cycle, which genes are cell-type) must be known exactly. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-count-simulation--ed455474c2d999ae))
- **scrnaseq-cross-group-ppt**: Use for scRNAseq cross-group PPTs with DA/DEG/GO/TE data. ([ShiganLuo/skill](https://github.com/ShiganLuo/skill/tree/HEAD/bioinformatics/scrnaseq-cross-group-ppt))
- **scrnaseq-cross-platform-benchmark-evaluation**: Use when several labeled scRNA-seq studies of the same tissue exist and you need to quantify how annotation accuracy transfers between studies/platforms (inter-study annotation) across competing classifiers. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-cross-platform-benchmark-evaluation))
- **scrnaseq-data-formatting**: Use when raw scRNA-seq data (e.g. GEO accession GSE116753 or similar) must be reshaped into MDIC3's exp.txt/metadata.txt input format before GRN inference with GNIPLR and communication scoring. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-data-formatting))
- **scrnaseq-data-input-format-preparation**: Use when scRNA-seq data with cell-type and time-point metadata must be formatted into the expression/cell-type/time-point files that dsCellNet requires before running datainput and downstream network inference. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-data-input-format-preparation))
- **scrnaseq-dataset-harmonization**: Use when several scRNA-seq datasets with expression matrices, dataset-of-origin labels, and cell-type labels must be combined to test whether cell types (e.g. neurons vs non-neuronal cells) replicate across studies via AUROC. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-dataset-harmonization))
- **scrnaseq-dataset-retrieval-geo-scp**: Use when a benchmark or method comparison requires raw scRNA-seq count matrices with ground-truth cell labels and the paper lists source repositories (GEO/SCP accession numbers, e.g. Table 1 of the scDHA article) to fetch them. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-dataset-retrieval-geo-scp))
- **scrnaseq-dataset-subsampling**: Use when you must test whether a scRNAseq clustering method's performance (e.g., ARI on Zheng68K) is robust to dataset size and cell-type complexity, by benchmarking on controlled subsampled datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/scrnaseq-dataset-subsampling))
- **scrnaseq-dataset-subsetting**: Use when benchmarking PAS/scoring tools on million-cell-level scRNA-seq data where full-dataset computation is impractical, and a fixed-size random cell subset (e.g., 100k cells from the &gt;1.2M-cell lupus atlas) suffices for validation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-dataset-subsetting))
- **scrnaseq-dataset-subsetting-and-downsampling**: Use when a large single-cell RNA-seq dataset (e.g. a whole-tumor immune atlas) must be narrowed to a biologically relevant population (immune cells) and a fixed number of cells (e.g. 5000) before per-cell scoring. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-dataset-subsetting-and-downsampling))
- **scrnaseq-deep-learning**: Use scVI, scANVI, totalVI, scGPT, Geneformer, and scFoundation for single-cell RNA-seq analysis. Use for batch correction, label transfer, perturbation prediction, and foundation model fine-tuning. Covers training, inference, GPU sizing, and evaluation metrics. ([pradyumnasagar/open-research-skills](https://github.com/pradyumnasagar/open-research-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/machine-learning-bio/scrnaseq-deep-learning) · ★8)
- **scrnaseq-doublet-detection-ensemble-analysis**: Use when processing droplet-based scRNA-seq data (Seurat/SingleCellExperiment objects) that lacks experimental doublet labels (no cell hashing, MULTI-seq, or demuxlet), and doublets must be called computationally per sample before downstream clustering or trajectory analyses. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-doublet-detection-ensemble-analysis))
- **scrnaseq-dropout-masking**: Use when benchmarking gene-imputation methods on scRNA-seq count matrices (h5 format) under controlled synthetic dropout, e.g. to verify a method recovers masked non-zero entries better than baselines. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-dropout-masking))
- **scrnaseq-dropout-masking-simulation**: Use when benchmarking an scRNA-seq imputation method on controlled dropout recovery — i.e., you have complete pre-processed count matrices and need to quantify NMSE/RMSE/MAE recovery at artificially masked non-zero values. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-dropout-masking-simulation))
- **scrnaseq-embedding-suitability-evaluation**: Use when fitting trajectories (e.g., Slingshot, Monocle3) on scRNA-seq data and you must decide among candidate embeddings from feature selection (number of highly variable genes) and dimension reduction choices, or need to know if a trajectory is appropriate at all. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-embedding-suitability-evaluation))
- **scrnaseq-entropy-model-implementation**: Use when implementing or validating an entropy-based cluster purity score for scRNA-seq clusters, i.e. computing single-gene sample entropy, LOESS-derived ds residuals, and aggregating them into ROGUE. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-entropy-model-implementation))
- **scrnaseq-expression-matrix-normalization**: Use when raw scRNA-seq count matrices for a labeled training set and a testing set (e.g., ASD snRNA-seq patients vs controls) must be normalized and restricted to their shared genes prior to annotation or benchmarking. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-expression-matrix-normalization))
- **scrnaseq-expression-matrix-permutation**: Use when you must verify that a matrix-imputation pipeline (e.g., SERM) is invariant to the arrangement of cells and genes — i.e., row/column permutations of a scRNA-seq count matrix yield near-identical Pearson correlations to the reference. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-expression-matrix-permutation))
- **scrnaseq-expression-matrix-processing**: Use when a single-cell scRNA-seq expression matrix (anndata object) and Step 1 cell phase posteriors are available and genes not yet labeled cyclers must be screened for de novo circadian rhythmicity before being added to the current cycler set. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-expression-matrix-processing))
- **scrnaseq-half-sample-resampling**: Use when comparing marker-selection methods (e.g. G-PC, CEM-PC, mRMR, ReliefF, F values) on scRNA-seq expression matrices and you need to quantify how reproducible each method's selected feature sets are across repeated random resampling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-half-sample-resampling))
- **scrnaseq-homogeneity-detection**: Use when a scRNA-seq dataset is about to undergo trajectory/pseudotime inference and you must first verify it actually contains a continuous trajectory (i.e., is not homogeneous cells or disconnected clusters). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-homogeneity-detection))
- **scrnaseq-imputation-benchmarking**: Use when comparing imputation methods on scRNA-seq count matrices to test whether imputation improves cell-type separation or preserves biological zeros, e.g. before-vs-after classification error on downsampled Zeisel/Baron/Chen/La Manno data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-imputation-benchmarking))
- **scrnaseq-latent-space-construction-from-target-sets**: Use when a scRNAseq counts table with cell-cluster assignments (e.g. setA, clusters 1-5) must be functionally annotated by reconstructing clusters in an encoded space derived from validated target/signature sets rather than gene expression alone. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-latent-space-construction-from-target-sets))
- **scrnaseq-library-size-offset-normalization**: Use when analyzing scRNA-seq count matrices (UMI or read counts) across cells with varying sequencing depth, and a Tweedie/GLM-style model requires a library-size offset log(S) instead of pre-normalizing counts. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-library-size-offset-normalization))
- **scrnaseq-log-normalized-matrix-handling**: Use when feeding raw perturbation (Y) and control (Z) cell-by-gene count matrices into scDecouple or similar scCRISPR-seq pipelines that require log-normalized, HVG-subtracted matrices as input. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-log-normalized-matrix-handling))
- **scrnaseq-log2-transformation-preprocessing**: Use when raw scRNA-seq count data (rows = cells, columns = genes) must be pre-processed and log2-transformed before dropout imputation or before measuring K-means cell-clustering accuracy with ARI. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-log2-transformation-preprocessing))
- **scrnaseq-marker-gene-identification**: Use when clustering a scRNA-seq dataset (e.g., Chen, Xin, Gierahn benchmarks) requires per-cluster marker genes with explicit fold-change, significance, and expression-prevalence cutoffs to drive automatic cell-type annotation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-marker-gene-identification))
- **scrnaseq-marker-selection-benchmarking**: Use when comparing marker-selection methods on scRNA-seq phenotype-classification tasks: you have count matrices with cell-type/phenotype labels and need discriminative marker sets plus downstream classifier F1 scores. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-marker-selection-benchmarking))
- **scrnaseq-mixed-transcriptional-profile-simulation**: Use when a paired scRNA-seq and spatial transcriptomics dataset is available and cell type proportions must be simulated with known ground truth to train or benchmark a deconvolution model (e.g., SpatialDDLS on 10x Visium/Chromium data). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-mixed-transcriptional-profile-simulation))
- **scrnaseq-multi-resolution-louvain-clustering**: Use when an scRNA-seq dataset must be clustered and there is no principled resolution choice: cluster across a resolution grid, compute marker specificity per configuration, and pick the resolution maximizing the specificity index. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-multi-resolution-louvain-clustering))
- **scrnaseq-neighborhood-scale-analysis**: Use when selecting a t-SNE perplexity/kEff for single-cell embeddings, or when characterizing whether scRNA-seq data have one or multiple intrinsic neighborhood scales via per-sample EMBEDR p-value sweeps. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-neighborhood-scale-analysis))
- **scrnaseq-normalized-expression-processing**: Use when constructing ligand-receptor communication edges from a normalised single-cell expression matrix and need an explicit, reproducible definition of gene 'expression' per cell type before edge extraction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-normalized-expression-processing))
- **scrnaseq-normalized-matrix-qc-processing**: Use when the input is a barcoded scRNA-seq dataset (e.g. GEO accession GSE99915) needing a QC-filtered, normalized expression matrix plus cell-ID-to-barcode-ID lineage annotations before lineage inference or memory-gene analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-normalized-matrix-qc-processing))

([majiayu000/claude-skill-registry](https://github.com/majiayu000/claude-skill-registry/tree/HEAD/skills/ai-ml/scrnaseq-pipeline-gptomics-bioskills-2) · ★745)
- **scrnaseq-post-clustering-qc**: Post-clustering QC: filter cells, detect doublets. ([ShiganLuo/skill](https://github.com/ShiganLuo/skill/tree/HEAD/bioinformatics/scrnaseq-post-clustering-qc))
- **scrnaseq-preprocessing-quality-control**: Use when raw scRNA-seq count data must be filtered (min/max genes per cell, min cells per gene, mitochondrial percentage, HVG dispersion) before single-cell drug-response prediction with scDEAL. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-preprocessing-quality-control))
- **scrnaseq-principal-component-analysis**: Use when evaluating whether denoising or imputation of scRNA-seq data preserves biological structure, by comparing principal component subspaces of original vs. perturbed/imputed matrices. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-principal-component-analysis))
- **scrnaseq-pseudotime-gene-expression-modeling**: Use when a sample-level trajectory (PILOT diffusion-map embedding + EIPLGraph pseudotime, e.g. from myocardial_infarction.h5ad) already exists and you must rank cell populations changing along progression and identify cluster-specific genes via Wald tests. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-pseudotime-gene-expression-modeling))
- **scrnaseq-quality-control-filtering**: Use when preprocessing 10X Genomics scRNA-seq count matrices (e.g., GEO samples GSM3453537/GSM3453538) before building a CellChat object, and cells must be filtered by UMI count, gene count, and mitochondrial fraction thresholds. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-quality-control-filtering--5719d268f788c843))
- **scrnaseq-quality-control-filtering**: Use when a raw counts matrix or Seurat object (csv/rds/h5 or 10X folder) enters a scRNA-seq workflow and cells must be filtered for quality before annotation, clustering, or gene-set scoring. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-quality-control-filtering--54323f323dcf3556))
- **scrnaseq-random-subsampling-pilot-design**: Use when planning scRNA-seq experiments from small pilot data: you need to test whether generative models trained on sub-sampled pilot datasets can produce synthetic data whose clustering, cell-type proportions, and per-gene distributions match a larger ground-truth dataset. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-random-subsampling-pilot-design))
- **scrnaseq-read-alignment-star**: Use when raw scRNA-seq reads (.sra/.fastq, e.g., from the Kim dataset) must be mapped to HUMAN reference (read_length 100) to produce BAMs feeding featureCounts and GATK-based SNV calling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-read-alignment-star))
- **scrnaseq-read-simulation**: Use when a study needs synthetic scRNA-seq reads (BAM/FASTQ) that mimic real data at both read-sequence and read-count levels, e.g., benchmarking UMI deduplication tools or comparing read-coverage fidelity against simulators like minnow. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-read-simulation))
- **scrnaseq-reference-based-deconvolution**: Use when spatial transcriptomics data (e.g., Visium spots with multi-cell content, or Slide-seq singlet/doublet spots) must be assigned cell-type labels using a paired, cell-type-annotated scRNA-seq/snRNA-seq reference, and accuracy must be benchmarked per slice vs. jointly across slices. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-reference-based-deconvolution))
- **scrnaseq-reference-based-proportion-estimation**: Use when you have bulk RNA-seq read counts from a complex tissue and a matching scRNA-seq reference with cell type annotations, and need cell type proportion estimates without pre-selected marker genes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-reference-based-proportion-estimation))
- **scrnaseq-reference-based-spot-deconvolution**: Use when a seq-based ST dataset (e.g., 10X Visium spots) needs per-spot cell type proportions estimated from a matched scRNA-seq reference, or when cell-type composition must be obtained before Splane spatial-domain identification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-reference-based-spot-deconvolution))
- **scrnaseq-reference-curation**: Use when deciding whether an available scRNA-seq reference (e.g., one missing key cell types like neurons, or an atlas from a different tissue such as mouse brain) is fit for reference-based deconvolution of multi-cellular pixel-resolution ST data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-reference-curation))
- **scrnaseq-reference-mapping**: Use when a query scRNA-seq (or spatial MERFISH) dataset must be annotated by transferring labels from a matched reference scRNA-seq atlas sharing the same tissue, especially when batch effects separate the two datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-reference-mapping))
- **scrnaseq-runtime-benchmarking**: Use when comparing computational scalability of rare-cell identification methods on scRNA-seq data, or when judging whether a method can process datasets in the tens-of-thousands-of-cells range. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-runtime-benchmarking))
- **scrnaseq-scanpy-core-analysis**: End-to-end scRNA-seq analysis in Python using Scanpy and the scverse stack — QC, normalization, integration, clustering, annotation, pseudobulk DE. Use when analyzing 10X / Drop-seq / Smart-seq data and you want a Python (not R) workflow. ([UViiVe/fleet-skills-public](https://github.com/UViiVe/fleet-skills-public/tree/HEAD/scrnaseq-scanpy-core-analysis))
- **scrnaseq-seurat-core-analysis**: End-to-end scRNA-seq analysis in R using Seurat v5 — QC, SCTransform, Harmony / CCA / RPCA integration, clustering, annotation, pseudobulk DE. Use when working in R/Bioconductor and the upstream stack is already Seurat-based. ([UViiVe/fleet-skills-public](https://github.com/UViiVe/fleet-skills-public/tree/HEAD/scrnaseq-seurat-core-analysis))
- **scrnaseq-similarity-matrix-visualization**: Use when you need to visually compare cell-type or developmental-stage separation produced by different learned cell-to-cell similarity matrices on scRNA-seq data, e.g. benchmarking SSRE against Corr, SIMLR, MPSSC, SSR on the Goolam or Yan datasets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-similarity-matrix-visualization))
- **scrnaseq-simulation-with-ground-truth-subclusters**: Use when validating or benchmarking a cluster-merging / cluster-significance method (e.g. Cytocipher cluster-merge) on simulated scRNA-seq data where the true subcluster structure is known and an AUROC against ground-truth labels is the target metric. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-simulation-with-ground-truth-subclusters))
- **scrnaseq-srt-cross-platform-comparison**: Use when a benchmark produces per-method accuracy scores computed separately on SRT and scRNA-seq reference datasets (e.g., accuracy_scores.csv) and you need to test cross-platform agreement of method rankings/scores. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/scrnaseq-srt-cross-platform-comparison))
- **scrnaseq-summary-statistics-construction**: Use when scRNA-seq count data are available and gene-level DE effect sizes (beta coefficients and their variances) must be generated as input for iDEA's joint DE and gene set enrichment analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-summary-statistics-construction))
- **scrnaseq-time-series-trajectory-inference**: Use when a large single-cell RNA-seq dataset with collection time-point labels needs a balanced subsample before trajectory inference, especially to exclude cells whose pseudotime disagrees with their collection time point. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scrnaseq-time-series-trajectory-inference))
- **scrnaseq-transfer-learning-clustering**: Use when clustering a small scRNA-Seq target dataset (e.g. rare cell types, 300–600 cells) for which a large, well-annotated source dataset from a related tissue or context exists. - **scrumia-author**: Brings a need to a ScrumIA module the anatomy check accepts on its first run — deciding whether a module is warranted at all, where it lives, and writing only what the module actually has. Use it to create a module, to change one, or to move one between locations, and before proposing one: two of its answers are that no module should exist. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-core/skills/scrumia-author) · ★1)
- **scrumia-brainstorm**: Scopes an idea or a brief through challenge, until it is ready to be split into features. Use it when opening a project, when a new feature arrives, or to critique an idea before committing. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-discovery/skills/scrumia-brainstorm) · ★1)
- **scrumia-compose**: Inspects, modifies, or diagnoses the module composition of a ScrumIA project. Use it to find out which module does what, plug in a new one, replace one, or understand why a capability is missing. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-core/skills/scrumia-compose) · ★1)
- **scrumia-design-audit**: Audits an existing interface against the design system — the drift (invented values, duplicated components) and, on equal footing, the mutedness (consistent but with no identity). Use it before a redesign, when plugging this module into an existing app, or to take stock after a phase of rapid growth. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-design/skills/scrumia-design-audit) · ★1)
- **scrumia-design-setup**: Installs the design system in a project — the design/ tree, its tokens and identity files, and the optional link to a Claude Design project. Invoked by scrumia-init when this module fills the design slot, or by hand to check the installation. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-design/skills/scrumia-design-setup) · ★1)
- **scrumia-design-sync**: Syncs the local design system with a claude.ai/design project through the DesignSync tool — component by component, never as a wholesale replace. Use it to publish a new or changed component for review, or to check what upstream has that the repo doesn't. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-design/skills/scrumia-design-sync) · ★1)
- **scrumia-design-system**: The ScrumIA design-system reference — where identity, tokens and components live, and how to consult them before writing an interface. Load it before creating or modifying a component, a style, or anything a user will look at. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-design/skills/scrumia-design-system) · ★1)
- **scrumia-effect**: The typed-effect discipline for an agent — describe before execute, failure as a value, the effect boundary, and which approach to reach for (Result, Either, IO/suspend, or effect.website). Library-agnostic on the pattern; cites effect.website as the reference implementation rather than wrapping it. Load before writing or reviewing code that produces, transforms, or consumes a side effect in an app where this module is plugged in. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-effect/skills/scrumia-effect) · ★1)
- **scrumia-extend**: The ScrumIA extension protocol — how a main skill opens a register, how any module contributes directives to it as pure data, and how scrumia-extends renders the table an agent picks from. Load it before writing or changing a module's extends.json, registers.json or dependencies.jsonl, or before adding an extension point to a skill. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-core/skills/scrumia-extend) · ★1)
- **scrumia-feature**: Creates, updates or audits a ScrumIA feature in features/. Applies the contextual angle catalog (index, business, qa, changelog, ux, tech, api-contract, archi, legal, security, devx) instead of a fixed template. Use it whenever a spec needs to be written, modified or checked. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-specs/skills/scrumia-feature) · ★1)
- **scrumia-functional-programming**: The ScrumIA Functional Programming paradigm — purity, total functions, referential transparency, immutability, composition over inheritance, and effect discipline, all in language-neutral terms so the same wording holds in Kotlin, JavaScript, Rust and any language that supports them. Load it before writing code in an app that extends this module. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-functional-programming/skills/scrumia-functional-programming) · ★1)
- **scrumia-gradle**: The ScrumIA Gradle reference — Kotlin DSL over Groovy, the version catalog as the one place versions live, the shape of a convention plugin (build-logic composite, precompiled script plugin), lazy task configuration, the build and configuration caches, composite builds for local siblings, pluginManagement, and documentation tasks wired into the lifecycle. Stated in terms of Gradle, not Kotlin Multiplatform. Load it before writing or reviewing a Gradle build script. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-gradle/skills/scrumia-gradle) · ★1)
- **scrumia-gradle-audit**: Audits an existing Gradle project against the eight rules this module ships — Kotlin DSL over Groovy, version catalog usage, convention plugin shape, lazy task configuration, the build and configuration caches, composite builds for local siblings, pluginManagement, and documentation tasks wired into the lifecycle. Use it before adopting the module on an existing codebase, when a build shape change ships, or to verify a project held to the rules really is. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-gradle/skills/scrumia-gradle-audit) · ★1)
- **scrumia-init**: Installs or verifies a project's ScrumIA composition — creates .scrumia/config.yaml, the specs tree, and writes into CLAUDE.md which modules are plugged in and where. Use it on first installation, or after adding or changing a module. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-core/skills/scrumia-init) · ★1)
- **scrumia-kotlin**: The ScrumIA Kotlin reference — naming and scope functions, null-safety and platform types, coroutines and Flow, data/sealed/value classes, object vs companion vs top-level, and visibility modifiers. Load it before writing Kotlin in an app whose language module is scrumia-kotlin, and read the refusal rules the same way on review. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-kotlin) · ★1)
- **scrumia-kotlin-audit**: Audit a Kotlin codebase against the six rule families this module ships — val/var and scope functions, null-safety and platform types, coroutines and Flow, data/sealed/value classes, object vs companion vs top-level, and visibility modifiers. Use it before adopting the module on an existing codebase, when a Kotlin bug ships, or to check a contract the language documents is actually being honoured. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-kotlin/skills/scrumia-kotlin-audit) · ★1)
- **scrumia-kotlin-multiplatform-mobile**: The Kotlin Multiplatform Mobile reference — six rule families that govern how KMP source sets, targets, Cocoapods integration and Gradle wiring compose into one multiplatform module. Use it before changing an expect/actual declaration, the source-set layout, the targets, a pod, or the KMP-shaped Gradle block. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-kotlin-multiplatform-mobile/skills/scrumia-kotlin-multiplatform-mobile) · ★1)
- **scrumia-material3**: The Material 3 entry point for an Android or Kotlin Multiplatform Mobile app — Compose is the default, Views is an explicit choice, and every token, component, theming and accessibility rule is stated as a falsifiable directive. Load before writing UI in an app that extends this module. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-material3) · ★1)
- **scrumia-module-audit**: Audits one ScrumIA module against the anatomy rules a program cannot decide from its tree alone — one concern per file, an index that routes rather than carries, and the README sections that depend on what a module actually does. Use it after `scrumia-module check`, or wherever a module's shape needs a verdict a reader would otherwise have to produce by hand. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-core/skills/scrumia-module-audit) · ★1)
- **scrumia-place**: Routes something learned during a run to exactly one destination — a module, this project, a feature, a ticket, the change itself, or agent memory. Use it when a run turns up something worth keeping, when an agent has just written to its own memory, or to re-place entries already sitting in a memory directory. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-core/skills/scrumia-place) · ★1)
- **scrumia-project-setup**: Sets up a project's GitHub tracking — board columns, labels, issue templates. Invoked by scrumia-init when this module fills the tracker slot, or by hand to check the installation. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-github-project/skills/scrumia-project-setup) · ★1)
- **scrumia-reactjs**: The ScrumIA React 19 reference — Server Components by default, Actions and useActionState for mutations and forms, derived state calculated during render, behaviour-first component tests, feature-based structure, and what we refuse. Load it before writing code in an app whose implementation module is scrumia-impl-reactjs. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-impl-reactjs/skills/scrumia-reactjs) · ★1)
- **scrumia-reactjs-audit**: Measures the gap between an existing React 19 app and scrumia-impl-reactjs rules — wrong client/server boundaries, useEffect for derived state, imperative DOM, components that fetch directly instead of going through the data boundary. Use it before plugging the module into an existing app, or to check that an app stays on the rails. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-impl-reactjs/skills/scrumia-reactjs-audit) · ★1)
- **scrumia-refine**: Refines a backlog ticket until it is ready for development — calls on the roles with the global view, splits into sub-issues when necessary, updates the specs. Use it to move a ticket from Backlog to Ready for dev. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-github-project/skills/scrumia-refine) · ★1)
- **scrumia-review**: Reviews a ScrumIA PR by routing to the right reviewers according to what it touches. Use it to review an open PR, or before approving a merge. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-github-project/skills/scrumia-review) · ★1)
- **scrumia-rules**: The rules-hierarchy format reference — section anatomy (index, guides, decisions, section.json), how an agent navigates it without reading everything, and when a single file is enough instead. Load it before reading or writing any module-shipped or project-local rule section. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-rules/skills/scrumia-rules) · ★1)
- **scrumia-rules-setup**: Scaffolds a project-local rule section under .scrumia/rules/&lt;section&gt;/ — short interview, then harvests existing conventions from the codebase instead of inventing them, writes the index/guides/decisions, and registers the section in CLAUDE.md. Use it when a project's own conventions on a topic are worth writing down as rules. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-rules/skills/scrumia-rules-setup) · ★1)
- **scrumia-rules-update**: Evolves an existing rule — locates its governing decision, challenges it in writing against the new context, updates decision and guides together, and logs the change in the section's CHANGELOG. Use whenever a rule needs to change, not just its guide. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-rules/skills/scrumia-rules-update) · ★1)
- **scrumia-rust**: The ScrumIA Rust reference — tests per invariant, unrepresentable invalid states, typed errors per layer, structure by domain, and what we refuse. Load it before writing code in an app whose implementation module is scrumia-impl-rust. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-impl-rust/skills/scrumia-rust) · ★1)
- **scrumia-rust-audit**: Measures the gap between an existing Rust app and the scrumia-impl-rust rules — unwrap in production, untyped errors, traits without polymorphism, unjustified unsafe. Use it before plugging the module into an existing app, or to check that a well-kept app stays that way. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-impl-rust/skills/scrumia-rust-audit) · ★1)
- **scrumia-solid-audit**: Audits an app's design against SOLID — the violations and, on equal footing, the over-applications (abstractions without variation, dead indirections). Use it to take stock of the design before a refactor or after a phase of rapid growth. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-solid-principles/skills/scrumia-solid-audit) · ★1)
- **scrumia-solid-principles**: The ScrumIA SOLID reference — the five principles, each with its limit of application, in OO and functional alike. Load it before writing or designing code in an app where this module is plugged in. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-solid-principles/skills/scrumia-solid-principles) · ★1)
- **scrumia-solid-refactor**: Resolves a design finding — SOLID violation or over-application — in safe, verifiable steps. Use it after scrumia-solid-audit, one finding at a time, never for a general compliance pass. ([tibs245/scrumia](https://github.com/tibs245/scrumia/tree/HEAD/plugins/scrumia-solid-principles/skills/scrumia-solid-refactor) · ★1)

- **scvelo**: - **Start with stochastic mode** for exploration; switch to dynamical for final analysis - **Need good coverage of unspliced reads**: Short reads (&lt; 100 bp) may miss intron coverage - **Minimum 2,000 cells**: RNA velocity is noisy with fewer cells - **Velocity should be coherent**: Arrows should follow known biology; randomness indicates issues - **k-NN bandwidth matters**: Too few neighbors → noi ([mkurman/zorai](https://github.com/mkurman/zorai/tree/HEAD/skills/scientific-skills/scvelo) · ★327)
- **scvelo**: scVelo is the leading Python package for RNA velocity analysis in single-cell RNA-seq data. It infers cell state transitions by modeling the kinetics of mRNA splicing — using the ratio of unspliced (pre-mRNA) to spliced (mature mRNA) abundances to determine whether a gene is being upregulated or downregulated in each cell. This allows reconstruction of developmental trajectories and identification ([ComeOnOliver/skillshub](https://github.com/ComeOnOliver/skillshub/tree/HEAD/skills/K-Dense-AI/claude-scientific-skills/scvelo) · ★67)

- **scvi-basic**: scVI — probabilistic deep generative model for scRNA-seq integration and batch correction. Use for unsupervised integration across batches or samples, building a latent space for downstream analysis, or as a preprocessing step before scANVI. Requires raw counts. For semi-supervised annotation use scvi-scanvi; for multiome use scvi-multivi. ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/scvi-basic) · ★1)
- **scvi-continuous-covariate-correction**: Use when single-cell RNA-seq data contain quantifiable continuous nuisance covariates (e.g., cell-cycle or sex scores) and a latent-space batch-correction baseline is needed to compare against gene-level filtering methods. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scvi-continuous-covariate-correction))
- **scvi-contrastivevi**: contrastiveVI from scvi-tools — a contrastive deep generative model that splits scRNA-seq variation into salient (target-specific) and background (shared) latent spaces. Use for Perturb-seq, drug-vs-control, or disease-vs-healthy comparisons where confounding variation masks the effect. Needs a clear target-vs-background split and raw counts. ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/scvi-contrastivevi) · ★1)
- **scvi-covariate-validation**: Validate scVI batch correction covariate choices by comparing latent space quality metrics ([smith6jt-cop/Skills_Registry](https://github.com/smith6jt-cop/Skills_Registry/tree/HEAD/plugins/scientific/scvi-covariate-validation/skills/scvi-covariate-validation) · ★4)
- **scvi-framework**: scvi-tools framework foundation and router — installation, a model-selection decision tree, and the shared setup_anndata / train / get_latent_representation contract. Use first when choosing among the scVI variants, or when a cross-cutting concern applies to any scvi-* child (raw counts, batch keys, scArches flags, hub loading, GPU). ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/scvi-framework) · ★1)
- **scvi-hub-models**: Browses and loads pretrained scvi-tools models from the Hugging Face scvi-hub, and picks which scvi variant fits a modality (RNA, CITE-seq, multiome, ATAC, perturbation). Use when loading a HubModel, fetching a reference atlas checkpoint, or minifying a model. It does no surgery — for mapping, hand off to scvi-scarches-reference-mapping. ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/scvi-hub-models) · ★1)
- **scvi-lda**: AmortizedLDA (scvi-tools) for topic modeling on raw scRNA-seq counts, treating cells as documents and genes as words to find discrete transcriptional programs and per-cell topic proportions. Use for unsupervised gene-module discovery and transitional states. Needs raw counts. For continuous signed loadings use scvi-linearscvi. ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/scvi-lda) · ★1)

- **scvi-linearscvi**: LinearSCVI (a scvi-tools variant with a linear decoder) on raw scRNA-seq counts, giving continuous signed gene-to-factor loadings via get_loadings() — interpretable factors showing which genes drive each latent dimension. Use as a count-aware replacement for PCA-on-logcounts. No scArches support. For discrete topics use scvi-lda. ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/scvi-linearscvi) · ★1)

- **scvi-mrvi**: MrVI from scvi-tools — multi-resolution variational inference for multi-donor scRNA-seq, modeling sample identity as biological rather than nuisance variation. Use for cohort studies needing cell-type-specific sample-to-sample distances and covariate-linked DE at single-cell resolution. sample_key is distinct from batch_key. ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/scvi-mrvi) · ★1)
- **scvi-multivi**: MultiVI jointly integrates paired and unpaired single-cell RNA and ATAC via scvi-tools, producing a shared latent embedding with cross-modality imputation. Use when combining 10x Multiome with RNA-only or ATAC-only datasets, or running DE plus DA on joint embeddings. For ATAC-only use scvi-peakvi. ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/scvi-multivi) · ★1)
- **scvi-peakvi**: PeakVI is a deep generative model in scvi-tools for scATAC-seq, producing batch-corrected latent embeddings and region-level differential accessibility. Use for standard scATAC clustering, batch correction across experiments, and scaling past a million cells. For paired RNA+ATAC use scvi-multivi; for TF activity use chromvar-motif-accessibility. ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/scvi-peakvi) · ★1)
- **scvi-prior-sampling-implementation**: Use when generating synthetic scRNA-seq observations with a trained VAE (scVI) but you want generation independent of the original observations, i.e., sampling directly from the prior of the latent space rather than the posterior. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/scvi-prior-sampling-implementation))
- **scvi-scanvi**: scANVI — semi-supervised deep generative model for scRNA-seq with partial cell type labels. Use when you have seed labels (full or partial) and need label transfer with better bio-conservation than scVI alone. Always initialize from a trained scVI model. For unsupervised integration use scvi-basic. ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/scvi-scanvi) · ★1)
- **scvi-scarches-reference-mapping**: scArches architectural surgery to map a query dataset onto an existing scvi-tools reference (scVI, scANVI, totalVI, MultiVI, trVAE): gene padding, adapter layers, fine-tuning with a frozen encoder, weighted-KNN label transfer, and novel-state detection. Use when the reference exists and you want to project queries without retraining. ([tony-zhelonkin/scio](https://github.com/tony-zhelonkin/scio/tree/HEAD/skills/scvi-scarches-reference-mapping) · ★1)
